+
+PRIMARY TEXT:
+
+#F3F3EF
+
+SECONDARY TEXT:
+
+#9B9B95
+
+MUTED TEXT:
+
+#676762
+
+HAIRLINES:
+
+rgba(255,255,255,0.08)
+
+STRONGER DIVIDERS:
+
+rgba(255,255,255,0.12)
+
+ACCENT:
+
+Electric acid green.
+
+Example:
+
+#B5FF00
+
+Use extremely sparingly.
+
+The accent should mean:
+
+ACTIVE
+CONNECTED
+LIVE
+OWNED BY TWINTHOS
+TRANSFERRED
+COMPLETE
+
+Never use green simply to decorate.
+
+============================================================
+THE MOST IMPORTANT VISUAL RULE
+============================================================
+
+BLACK SPACE MUST FEEL DESIGNED.
+
+Never allow black space to become:
+
+blank
+dead
+unfinished
+empty
+forgotten
+
+Minimalism is not:
+
+less content.
+
+Minimalism is:
+
+fewer, stronger elements.
+
+Every large empty region must be held by:
+
+typography
+alignment
+a line
+a system
+a proportion
+a visual anchor
+a motion state
+a separator
+a compositional relationship
+
+============================================================
+NO CALCULATOR
+============================================================
+
+Do not add:
+
+ROI calculator
+savings calculator
+slider tools
+input widgets
+fake dashboards
+interactive number toys
+
+These make the site feel like SaaS lead-generation software.
+
+Twinthos should communicate economic value through:
+
+real workflow logic
+real operational structure
+real audit artefacts
+measured client outcomes when available
+clear business-case language
+
+Use sophistication, not widgets.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR SYSTEM
+============================================================
+
+SEPARATORS ARE NOW A CORE PART OF THE DESIGN.
+
+Use separators to create rhythm between major ideas.
+
+Do not rely on giant empty black gaps.
+
+There should be multiple separator types.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 01
+HAIRLINE
+============================================================
+
+A single 1px horizontal line:
+
+rgba(255,255,255,0.08)
+
+Use between major editorial sections.
+
+Do not always span full width.
+
+Some lines should:
+
+start at grid column 2
+end at grid column 11
+
+Others may span full viewport.
+
+Create variation.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 02
+SYSTEM LINE
+============================================================
+
+Use thin architectural lines with small labels.
+
+Example:
+
+──────────────────────────
+01 / THE PROBLEM
+──────────────────────────
+
+or:
+
+THE PROBLEM
+────────────────────────────
+
+Keep labels very small.
+
+11–12px.
+
+Uppercase.
+
+Wide letter spacing.
+
+This creates a technical editorial feeling.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 03
+TRANSITION STATEMENT
+============================================================
+
+Instead of empty space, use a single line of transitional copy.
+
+Example:
+
+MORE SOFTWARE DID NOT CREATE LESS WORK.
+
+or:
+
+THE SYSTEMS CHANGED.
+THE HUMAN WORK REMAINED.
+
+or:
+
+THE WORK WAS NEVER REMOVED.
+IT WAS MOVED BETWEEN SYSTEMS.
+
+Place this between sections as a bridge.
+
+It should be approximately:
+
+24–32px desktop
+20–24px mobile
+
+Muted but visible.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 04
+INDEX MARKER
+============================================================
+
+Use section indexing occasionally.
+
+01
+02
+03
+04
+
+Very small.
+
+Positioned at left edge of composition.
+
+This creates structure and direction.
+
+Example:
+
+01
+THE PROBLEM
+
+02
+THE HANDOVER
+
+03
+THE CONTROL LAYER
+
+Do not number everything.
+
+Use only where useful.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 05
+VERTICAL AXIS
+============================================================
+
+Use a thin vertical line running through selected sections.
+
+This can represent:
+
+workflow
+handover
+continuity
+system state
+
+Sections can attach to this line.
+
+For example:
+
+EMAIL
+|
+CRM
+|
+CALENDAR
+|
+FILES
+|
+TWINTHOS
+
+The vertical axis can continue through more than one section.
+
+This gives continuity across scroll.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 06
+MATERIAL SHIFT
+============================================================
+
+Instead of a line:
+
+transition from:
+
+#050505
+
+to
+
+#090909
+
+then back.
+
+Extremely subtle.
+
+This can separate:
+
+belief section
+proof section
+offer section
+
+without introducing cards.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR TYPE 07
+TYPOGRAPHIC BLOCK
+============================================================
+
+A separator can simply be:
+
+CHECK.
+COPY.
+CHASE.
+UPDATE.
+
+Each word appearing across the width.
+
+Then:
+
+REMOVED.
+
+This can transition into the next section.
+
+============================================================
+PAGE RHYTHM
+============================================================
+
+The page must have rhythm.
+
+Use:
+
+STATEMENT
+↓
+SYSTEM
+↓
+SEPARATOR
+↓
+EXPLANATION
+↓
+PROOF
+↓
+SEPARATOR
+↓
+STATEMENT
+↓
+SYSTEM
+↓
+OFFER
+
+Never:
+
+BIG TEXT
+↓
+BIG TEXT
+↓
+BIG TEXT
+↓
+BIG TEXT
+
+Never:
+
+CARD
+↓
+CARD
+↓
+CARD
+
+Never:
+
+BLACK GAP
+↓
+BLACK GAP
+↓
+BLACK GAP
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 01
+HERO
+============================================================
+
+LEFT SIDE:
+
+Label:
+
+MANAGED OPERATIONS
+
+Headline:
+
+YOUR PEOPLE
+WEREN'T HIRED
+TO OPERATE SOFTWARE.
+
+Supporting copy:
+
+Twinthos takes over the repetitive work between your systems — checking, chasing, updating and moving information — so your team can focus on work that actually requires them.
+
+Primary CTA:
+
+Find the work we can remove
+
+Microcopy:
+
+No new platform.
+No new dashboard.
+Built around the systems you already use.
+
+RIGHT SIDE:
+
+Create a system composition.
+
+EMAIL
+CRM
+CALENDAR
+FILES
+ACCOUNTING
+PORTALS
+
+Between them:
+
+CHECK
+COPY
+UPDATE
+CHASE
+VERIFY
+FOLLOW UP
+
+Use typography and thin lines only.
+
+No boxes if possible.
+
+No large rounded cards.
+
+As the page loads or scrolls:
+
+human-work labels appear first.
+
+Then one by one they become muted.
+
+A TWINTHOS operational line remains.
+
+The hero must feel balanced.
+
+Do not leave 40% of the viewport as blank black.
+
+============================================================
+FIRST SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Directly after hero:
+
+full-width thin rule.
+
+Then small label:
+
+THE PROBLEM / 01
+
+Then a transitional statement:
+
+MORE SOFTWARE DID NOT CREATE LESS WORK.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 02
+THE PROBLEM
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+SOFTWARE MULTIPLIED.
+
+Second line:
+
+WORK DIDN'T DISAPPEAR.
+
+Use split composition.
+
+Left:
+
+large headline.
+
+Right:
+
+stack of system names.
+
+EMAIL
+CRM
+CALENDAR
+ACCOUNTING
+FILES
+PORTALS
+
+Between them show human actions.
+
+CHECK
+COPY
+MOVE
+CHASE
+VERIFY
+UPDATE
+
+Do not use icons.
+
+Do not use cards.
+
+Use text as system architecture.
+
+============================================================
+MICRO-SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Thin line.
+
+Then:
+
+PEOPLE BECAME THE GLUE.
+
+This statement should span significant width.
+
+Do not add body text immediately beneath.
+
+Let the line breathe.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 03
+THE GLUE
+============================================================
+
+Use an editorial grid.
+
+LEFT COLUMN:
+
+CHECK
+
+Small copy:
+
+Open it.
+Find it.
+Confirm it.
+
+RIGHT COLUMN:
+
+CHASE
+
+Small copy:
+
+Send it.
+Wait.
+Follow up.
+
+Next row:
+
+COPY
+
+From here.
+Into there.
+
+UPDATE
+
+Change one system.
+Then another.
+
+MOVE
+
+Information between tools.
+
+VERIFY
+
+Make sure it worked.
+
+Do NOT put these inside cards.
+
+Use grid lines as structure.
+
+This is where separators become visible architectural elements.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION DIVIDER
+============================================================
+
+Use a full-width line.
+
+At the far left:
+
+02 / WHY
+
+At far right:
+
+HUMAN INTELLIGENCE
+
+Then next section.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 04
+THE WHY
+============================================================
+
+This section may have more negative space.
+
+Headline:
+
+HUMAN INTELLIGENCE
+SHOULD NOT BE SPENT
+OPERATING SOFTWARE.
+
+Below:
+
+DECIDE
+
+CREATE
+
+NEGOTIATE
+
+LEAD
+
+SOLVE
+
+Each word should appear across a horizontal plane.
+
+Do not use animations that feel theatrical.
+
+Slow fade or directional reveal only.
+
+Supporting statement:
+
+Machines should handle the repeatable work around those decisions.
+
+This is the emotional centre.
+
+============================================================
+TRANSITION
+============================================================
+
+Use separator copy:
+
+THE WORK DOES NOT NEED TO DISAPPEAR FROM THE BUSINESS.
+
+IT NEEDS TO DISAPPEAR FROM THE PERSON.
+
+This is a strong conceptual bridge.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 05
+WATCH THE WORK DISAPPEAR
+============================================================
+
+Create one continuous process.
+
+CUSTOMER ENQUIRY
+|
+READ
+|
+CHECK RECORD
+|
+CHECK CALENDAR
+|
+UPDATE CRM
+|
+SEND CONFIRMATION
+|
+FOLLOW UP
+|
+COMPLETE
+
+Initially:
+
+human actions are visible.
+
+During scroll:
+
+READ
+CHECK
+UPDATE
+SEND
+FOLLOW UP
+
+shift to a TWINTHOS state.
+
+Use accent green sparingly to indicate ownership transfer.
+
+Do not animate every word.
+
+The process should feel mechanical.
+
+At the end:
+
+HUMAN INVOLVEMENT:
+
+EXCEPTION ONLY
+
+This should be illustrative unless based on a real workflow.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Thin rule.
+
+Small label:
+
+FROM MANUAL COORDINATION
+
+center:
+
+TO MANAGED OPERATION
+
+Then next section.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 06
+HOW IT WORKS
+============================================================
+
+Do not use four cards.
+
+Use one horizontal or vertical timeline.
+
+OBSERVE
+DEFINE
+HAND OVER
+OPERATE
+
+Connect them with a thin line.
+
+01 OBSERVE
+
+We map how the work actually happens.
+
+02 DEFINE
+
+We establish rules, permissions and human boundaries.
+
+03 HAND OVER
+
+Twinthos assumes the repeatable parts.
+
+04 OPERATE
+
+The workflow runs, exceptions are handled and performance is monitored.
+
+Use subtle progression as user scrolls.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 07
+THE OPERATING LAYER
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+YOUR BUSINESS ALREADY HAS
+AN OPERATING SYSTEM.
+
+IT'S JUST MOSTLY HUMAN.
+
+Below:
+
+PEOPLE
+
+SYSTEMS
+
+INFORMATION
+
+DECISIONS
+
+ACTIONS
+
+Use fine lines between each.
+
+Then introduce:
+
+TWINTHOS
+
+as the layer running through them.
+
+Supporting copy:
+
+Twinthos learns how these elements interact, then takes responsibility for the repeatable work between them.
+
+Do not over-explain.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Use a material shift.
+
+Background:
+
+#050505
+
+becomes:
+
+#090909
+
+Tiny top rule.
+
+Label:
+
+THE HANDOVER
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 08
+THE AUDIT
+============================================================
+
+This must feel like a serious operational document.
+
+Not a landing-page card.
+
+Use:
+
+large dark document surface
+sharp or very small radius
+thin border
+editorial table structure
+
+Header:
+
+TWINTHOS
+
+OPERATIONS AUDIT
+
+REDACTED CLIENT
+
+Then:
+
+WORKFLOW
+Enquiry → Appointment
+
+SYSTEMS
+Email
+CRM
+Calendar
+
+MANUAL MOVEMENTS
+Read
+Check
+Update
+Follow up
+
+AUTOMATION CANDIDATE
+Repeatable coordination
+
+HUMAN BOUNDARY
+Exceptions
+
+NEXT STEP
+Controlled deployment
+
+Use only real information.
+
+If information is unknown:
+
+do not invent values.
+
+============================================================
+ECONOMIC VALUE WITHOUT A CALCULATOR
+============================================================
+
+Do not use interactive inputs.
+
+Use a conceptual business-case section.
+
+Headline:
+
+REMOVE THE WORK
+BEFORE ADDING MORE PEOPLE.
+
+Then:
+
+CURRENT STATE
+
+People coordinate systems manually.
+
+↓
+
+TWINTHOS STATE
+
+Repeatable work moves to the operational layer.
+
+↓
+
+RETURN
+
+Time returns to the team.
+
+Then:
+
+THE BUSINESS CASE IS MEASURED DURING THE AUDIT.
+
+Small copy:
+
+We measure frequency, time, systems involved and human effort before recommending deployment.
+
+This feels sophisticated and credible.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Full width thin line.
+
+At left:
+
+CONTROL
+
+At right:
+
+AUTHORITY STAYS HUMAN
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 09
+CONTROL
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+AUTONOMOUS INSIDE
+THE BOUNDARIES YOU SET.
+
+Create two vertical fields.
+
+LEFT:
+
+TWINTHOS
+
+approved workflows
+approved systems
+approved communications
+repeatable actions
+
+RIGHT:
+
+HUMAN
+
+judgement
+approval
+exception
+strategic decision
+
+Between them:
+
+thin vertical separator.
+
+Label:
+
+ESCALATION BOUNDARY
+
+This should be one of the most elegant sections on the website.
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 10
+THE COMMERCIAL LOGIC
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+DON'T BUY AI.
+
+REMOVE WORK.
+
+Below:
+
+FIND
+
+the repetitive work.
+
+MEASURE
+
+what it costs.
+
+HAND OVER
+
+what is repeatable.
+
+KEEP
+
+what requires judgement.
+
+This replaces any cheap calculator-style section.
+
+============================================================
+SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Strong architectural divider.
+
+Approximately 1px.
+
+Spacing before and after should be controlled.
+
+Left:
+
+MANAGED OPERATIONS
+
+Right:
+
+THE OFFER
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 11
+THE OFFER
+============================================================
+
+Use large typography.
+
+No pricing card.
+
+No gradient.
+
+No glowing box.
+
+MANAGED OPERATIONS
+
+£5,000 / MONTH
+
+Minimum 3 months.
+
+Included:
+
+Workflow design
+Connections
+Deployment
+Monitoring
+Maintenance
+Exception handling
+Continuous improvement
+Support
+
+Then large space.
+
+Thin divider.
+
+START WITH THE AUDIT.
+
+£999
+
+Credited against month one if you proceed.
+
+CTA:
+
+Assess your operation
+
+============================================================
+SECTION 12
+ACCOUNTABILITY
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+THE PERSON DESIGNING
+YOUR OPERATION
+STAYS RESPONSIBLE FOR IT.
+
+Supporting copy:
+
+Twinthos deployments are currently founder-led from workflow design through live operation.
+
+No anonymous handover.
+
+No unnecessary layers between the client and the person responsible for the workflow.
+
+Keep this concise.
+
+============================================================
+FINAL SEPARATOR
+============================================================
+
+Use a distinctive final divider.
+
+At left:
+
+WORK REMOVED
+
+At centre:
+
+TIME RETURNED
+
+At right:
+
+HUMAN CONTROL
+
+Then final section.
+
+============================================================
+FINAL CTA
+============================================================
+
+Headline:
+
+WHAT SHOULD
+YOUR TEAM
+STOP DOING?
+
+Supporting copy:
+
+Show us one repetitive process.
+
+We'll identify what can be handed over, what should remain human and whether the process is worth changing.
+
+CTA:
+
+Assess your operation
+
+Microcopy:
+
+15 minutes.
+One workflow.
+No obligation.
+
+Footer immediately after.
+
+Do not create giant dead space after the CTA.
+
+============================================================
+NAVIGATION
+============================================================
+
+Use one global navigation everywhere.
+
+TWINTHOS
+
+How it works
+Use cases
+Audit
+Security
+About
+
+CTA:
+
+Assess your operation
+
+Nav should feel almost invisible.
+
+Transparent or near-black.
+
+Hairline border only after scroll.
+
+============================================================
+FOOTER
+============================================================
+
+Keep extremely restrained.
+
+TWINTHOS
+
+Work handled.
+Time returned.
+
+How it works
+Use cases
+Audit
+Security
+About
+Contact
+
+© 2026 Twinthos
+
+No giant newsletter form.
+
+No giant social icon row.
+
+No oversized footer.
+
+============================================================
+SHAPE LANGUAGE
+============================================================
+
+Avoid excessive roundness.
+
+2025–2026 AI design overused:
+
+rounded cards
+pills
+capsules
+soft blobs
+
+Twinthos should feel more architectural.
+
+Use:
+
+straight lines
+sharp alignment
+small-radius surfaces
+thin dividers
+strong vertical axes
+
+Suggested radius:
+
+0px
+4px
+6px
+
+Rarely exceed 12px.
+
+Buttons may use moderate radius, but avoid pill buttons everywhere.
+
+============================================================
+GRID
+============================================================
+
+Use a disciplined 12-column desktop grid.
+
+Maximum content width:
+
+approximately 1360px.
+
+Keep consistent left/right anchors across the page.
+
+Important:
+
+many expensive websites feel expensive because everything aligns.
+
+Alignment is a luxury signal.
+
+============================================================
+TYPE SCALE
+============================================================
+
+Hero:
+
+80–104px desktop.
+
+Large manifesto:
+
+68–88px.
+
+Major section:
+
+48–64px.
+
+Supporting headline:
+
+28–36px.
+
+Body:
+
+18–20px.
+
+Labels:
+
+11–13px.
+
+Metrics or price:
+
+80–120px where appropriate.
+
+Do not use tiny explanatory copy.
+
+============================================================
+ENGAGEMENT WITHOUT GIMMICKS
+============================================================
+
+Engagement should come from:
+
+narrative progression
+motion that explains ownership
+changing system states
+typographic scale
+section transitions
+workflow transformation
+visual hierarchy
+subtle hover behaviour
+real proof
+
+NOT:
+
+calculators
+gamification
+animated blobs
+fake dashboards
+scroll tricks
+3D gimmicks
+cursor gimmicks
+
+============================================================
+MOTION SYSTEM
+============================================================
+
+Motion must communicate a state change.
+
+Examples:
+
+MANUAL → TWINTHOS
+
+UNCONNECTED → CONNECTED
+
+ACTIVE → COMPLETE
+
+NORMAL → ESCALATED
+
+Use:
+
+opacity
+line growth
+slow translation
+mask reveals
+colour state changes
+
+Avoid:
+
+bouncing
+elastic motion
+random floating
+constant background movement
+excessive parallax
+
+============================================================
+SCROLL DESIGN
+============================================================
+
+Do not make every section 100vh.
+
+Use variable-height scenes.
+
+Some:
+
+450–600px.
+
+Some:
+
+700–900px.
+
+Major manifesto moments:
+
+90svh maximum.
+
+This creates rhythm.
+
+============================================================
+BLACK SPACE RULE
+============================================================
+
+Audit every viewport.
+
+Ask:
+
+IS THE SPACE ACTIVE?
+
+If the answer is no:
+
+fix it.
+
+Possible fixes:
+
+move content upward
+increase scale
+add separator
+use a vertical line
+add system structure
+merge sections
+change alignment
+create material shift
+
+Do not fix emptiness by adding generic copy.
+
+============================================================
+RED TEAM PASS
+============================================================
+
+Attack the website.
+
+QUESTION:
+
+Does this look like a generic AI company?
+
+If yes:
+strip it back.
+
+Does this look like SaaS?
+
+If yes:
+remove widgets.
+
+Does this feel empty?
+
+If yes:
+increase structural density.
+
+Does this feel busy?
+
+If yes:
+remove decoration.
+
+Does this feel like £5,000/month?
+
+If not:
+improve proof and precision.
+
+Does anything feel fabricated?
+
+If yes:
+delete it.
+
+Does any section exist only because websites normally have that section?
+
+If yes:
+delete it.
+
+============================================================
+BLUE TEAM PASS
+============================================================
+
+Protect:
+
+black minimalism
+large typography
+operational-drag narrative
+human-control story
+plain English
+restraint
+founder accountability
+audit-first commercial model
+
+Do not redesign away the strongest ideas.
+
+============================================================
+VISUAL QUALITY LOOP
+============================================================
+
+Run at least seven passes.
+
+PASS 01
+COMPOSITION
+
+Zoom to 25%.
+
+Ignore copy.
+
+Does the page look intentionally composed?
+
+PASS 02
+SPACE
+
+Scroll slowly.
+
+Find every dead black zone.
+
+Fix each one.
+
+PASS 03
+TYPOGRAPHY
+
+Check headline line-breaks manually.
+
+Never allow accidental awkward wrapping.
+
+PASS 04
+ALIGNMENT
+
+Check every grid anchor.
+
+Luxury comes from precision.
+
+PASS 05
+SEPARATORS
+
+Check whether section changes are clear but subtle.
+
+The separators should guide, not decorate.
+
+PASS 06
+MOBILE
+
+Design at:
+
+375px
+390px
+430px
+
+Do not simply collapse desktop.
+
+PASS 07
+SUBTRACTION
+
+Remove at least 10–15% of remaining visual elements.
+
+Then reassess.
+
+============================================================
+THE DEEPER VISUAL STORY
+============================================================
+
+The entire website should move from:
+
+FRAGMENTATION
+
+to:
+
+ORDER.
+
+At the top:
+
+many systems
+many actions
+many movements
+many responsibilities
+
+Through the middle:
+
+ownership transfers
+
+manual work disappears
+
+systems become coordinated
+
+By the bottom:
+
+only human judgement remains visible.
+
+This is the narrative.
+
+============================================================
+THE BRAND MOTION IDEA
+============================================================
+
+Twinthos should own one simple visual concept:
+
+WORK DISAPPEARS.
+
+Not AI appears.
+
+Not technology glows.
+
+Not robots arrive.
+
+WORK DISAPPEARS.
+
+CHECK disappears.
+
+COPY disappears.
+
+CHASE disappears.
+
+UPDATE disappears.
+
+VERIFY disappears.
+
+What remains:
+
+DECISION
+JUDGEMENT
+LEADERSHIP
+HUMAN CONTROL
+
+This is the strongest visual metaphor for the brand.
+
+============================================================
+FINAL EXPERIENCE STANDARD
+============================================================
+
+The visitor should experience:
+
+1.
+THIS IS DIFFERENT.
+
+2.
+I UNDERSTAND THE PROBLEM.
+
+3.
+I RECOGNISE THIS PROBLEM IN MY COMPANY.
+
+4.
+I UNDERSTAND WHAT TWINTHOS REMOVES.
+
+5.
+I UNDERSTAND WHY HUMANS STAY IN CONTROL.
+
+6.
+THIS FEELS SERIOUS.
+
+7.
+THIS FEELS EXPENSIVE FOR A REASON.
+
+8.
+I WANT TO KNOW WHAT TWINTHOS COULD REMOVE FROM MY BUSINESS.
+
+The experience must feel:
+
+CALM
+PRECISE
+STRUCTURED
+MODERN
+POWERFUL
+
+Never:
+
+FLASHY
+CHEAP
+TREND-CHASING
+AI-GENERATED
+TEMPLATE-DRIVEN
+
+The final design principle:
+
+DO NOT FILL SPACE.

# 2026-09-27 19:26:24.712948
+HERMES — TWINTHOS WEBSITE REVISION BRIEF
+
+Rework the website so a prospective client immediately understands:
+• What Twinthos handles.
+• What they receive.
+• What remains their responsibility.
+• Why ongoing management matters.
+• What happens when they enquire.
+
+The creative direction is premium, precise and confident: black, warm white, selective emerald, excellent typography and purposeful motion. Every visual effect should help the visitor understand the service.
+
+1. FIX THE FIRST SCREEN
+
+Observed problem:
+At a desktop viewport of approximately 1363 × 936, the current headline alone measured approximately 876px tall. The main hero CTA began around 1316px down the page. The headline overwhelms the explanation and pushes the decision below the first screen.
+
+It also spends the strongest position describing unfinished work rather than introducing the solution.
+
+Replace the hero with:
+
+Eyebrow:
+MANAGED AI FOR BUSINESS OPERATIONS
+
+Headline:
+The work moves forward.
+You stop chasing it.
+
+Supporting copy:
+Twinthos builds and manages AI agents that handle enquiries, follow-ups and routine admin across your business tools. Agreed work gets handled. Decisions that need your judgement come back to you.
+
+Primary CTA:
+Request a 15-minute fit call
+
+Secondary CTA:
+Watch a workflow
+
+Commercial line:
+£5,000/month for the agreed managed service. Implementation and ongoing management included. Start with one defined workflow.
+
+Show applicable VAT information close to the price, consistently with the actual terms.
+
+Use a compact two-column desktop hero: proposition and CTAs on the left; one compelling workflow demonstration on the right. Keep the headline, explanation and primary CTA visible together at ordinary laptop sizes. On mobile, put the proposition and CTA before the demonstration.
+
+Do not force this through tiny type or a rigid viewport height. Shorten copy and control headline width.
+
+2. MAKE THE PURCHASE TANGIBLE
+
+The current service can sound like £5,000/month for a booking automation. The site must explain the operational responsibility included in that fee.
+
+Introduce this section immediately after the opening demonstration:
+
+Heading:
+A defined job. A working agent. Ongoing management.
+
+Introduction:
+We agree the work, connect the tools and build the workflow. Then we monitor, maintain and manage it as part of the service.
+
+Present four concrete deliverables:
+
+Your workflow
+A written scope covering the work, systems, permitted actions and points requiring approval.
+
+Your working agent
+A configured workflow connected to the agreed tools and tested before activation.
+
+Your operating record
+A clear account of completed work, exceptions and requests needing a decision.
+
+Your managed service
+Monitoring, maintenance, agreed rule changes and direct support.
+
+Validate the reporting and support descriptions against what Twinthos actually delivers. Do not imply a client dashboard, instant support or continuous human supervision unless those exist.
+
+Explain what the client supplies: access, accurate rules and timely decisions on exceptions.
+
+3. TURN THE DEMO INTO THE MAIN SALES ARGUMENT
+
+Observed problems:
+The current demo mainly replaces paragraphs of text. It begins with a property-viewing enquiry, then jumps into an invoice-discount example. It ends with an approval pending while simultaneously announcing completion. Clicking the review control produced no visible response in the audit.
+
+This undermines the central promise: dependable work within clear boundaries.
+
+Build one coherent, approximately 15-second illustrative sequence:
+
+0–3 seconds — Work arrives
+A readable enquiry card appears:
+“Can we view Albert Road on Thursday evening?”
+
+3–6 seconds — Twinthos checks
+That same enquiry becomes an active work item. Show the relevant availability and rule being checked.
+
+6–10 seconds — The agreed action happens
+Include the customer’s selection or confirmation before creating the booking. Show a calendar event, confirmation message and updated customer record.
+
+10–13 seconds — Show the finished work
+Resolve into a calm summary:
+Viewing confirmed
+Customer record updated
+Team notified
+
+13–15 seconds — Leave the benefit visible
+“From enquiry to confirmed viewing.”
+“All steps shown use sample data.”
+
+These timings describe the presentation, not a claim about real processing speed.
+
+Keep the final result on screen. Offer Replay and a separate “See an exception” control.
+
+For the exception, remain in the same story: the customer requests a viewing outside approved hours. The agent prepares an approval request and pauses before confirming anything.
+
+Show:
+“Outside approved viewing hours.”
+“Awaiting your decision.”
+“No booking confirmed.”
+
+Do not announce completion while approval is outstanding.
+
+Use one consistent work item throughout. Make its movement between enquiry, checks and outputs visually traceable. The viewer should understand the result with the sound off and without reading a paragraph at every step.
+
+Keep the demo clearly labelled as illustrative. Every control must function.
+
+4. RESOLVE THE OFFER CONTRADICTIONS
+
+The homepage says “Not a pilot. A service.”
+Elsewhere it refers to a pilot commitment.
+The FAQ promises a pilot that ends without a managed-service charge unless the customer opts in.
+The terms describe a monthly service paid in advance.
+
+Resolve this against the actual commercial arrangement before publishing revised copy. Do not choose a policy merely because it converts better.
+
+Create one authoritative offer definition covering:
+• Whether there is a pilot.
+• Whether it is paid.
+• When monthly billing begins.
+• Initial scope and expansion.
+• Notice period.
+• Third-party costs.
+• VAT.
+• Support coverage.
+• The audit credit and its eligibility period.
+
+Use that definition consistently across the homepage, FAQ, booking journey, audit page and terms.
+
+The current audit terms also use a vague eligibility period for the credit. Replace that with the actual agreed period.
+
+Keep unresolved business decisions in internal implementation notes, never as contradictory customer-facing promises.
+
+5. MAKE THE EVIDENCE CREDIBLE
+
+The live homepage presents 247 enquiries, 1,180 follow-ups and 23 escalations as real results, alongside a zero-missed claim.
+
+Verify these against underlying records before retaining them. Check the period, sample, definition, exclusions and permission to publish. They may be valid; the website alone does not establish that.
+
+If verified, replace disconnected counters with one compact case study:
+• The original operational problem.
+• The agreed workflow.
+• What Twinthos handled.
+• What required a person.
+• The measured result over a stated period.
+
+Distinguish activity from outcomes. Sending a follow-up is not evidence of a sale. Handling an enquiry is not automatically a confirmed booking.
+
+If evidence is unavailable, remove the numerical claims and use the clearly labelled illustrative workflow.
+
+Do not place a “Live” status beside sample data in a way that suggests the demonstration is a real customer feed.
+
+6. SHORTEN AND REORDER THE PAGE
+
+Recommended sequence:
+
+1. Clear proposition and visual example.
+2. Concrete deliverables.
+3. Workflow options.
+4. Verified case study, if available.
+5. Three-step implementation process.
+6. Pricing and scope.
+7. Essential questions and final CTA.
+
+Consolidate the repeated explanations of scope, rules, management and getting started.
+
+Suggested workflow copy:
+
+Enquiries → confirmed next steps
+Handle incoming enquiries, coordinate availability and keep the customer record current.
+
+Leads → timely follow-ups
+Follow up within the agreed sequence and bring replies needing judgement to your team.
+
+Invoices → organised administration
+Check, match, record and chase within your approval rules.
+
+Client updates → consistent records
+Keep agreed information and routine notifications moving between your systems.
+
+Suggested process:
+
+Define the work
+Choose a recurring workflow with a clear outcome.
+
+Agree the boundaries
+Document the tools, permitted actions and decisions requiring approval.
+
+Build and manage
+Connect, test and run the workflow, then maintain it and report on its operation.
+
+Remove defensive commentary about sales pitches, menus, account managers and pilot programmes. Use that space to explain what the customer receives.
+
+7. GIVE THE DESIGN STRONGER HIERARCHY
+
+Preserve the dark visual identity, but improve legibility and composition.
+
+• Short headlines with controlled line breaks.
+• Body copy around 17–19px on desktop and 16–18px on mobile.
+• Comfortable reading widths.
+• Brighter explanatory text.
+• Small uppercase monospace reserved for brief labels.
+• Emerald used for primary actions and meaningful workflow states.
+• Consistent spacing based on content, without large empty black intervals.
+• One visual focal point per section.
+• Clear distinctions between process, proof, deliverables and price.
+
+The current #666 text on black is too subdued for small, important information. Measure contrast and improve it where needed.
+
+Premium design should make the content easier to absorb.
+
+8. ADD SLEEK, PURPOSEFUL MOTION
+
+Use a restrained motion system:
+
+Opening:
+Render the headline and CTA immediately. Let the adjacent workflow assemble in a short 400–600ms sequence. Do not hide the entire hero while waiting for the page load event.
+
+Section entrances:
+A single 8–12px rise with a 350–500ms fade. Trigger once. Keep essential content available if JavaScript fails.
+
+Workflow transitions:
+Use shared positions and smooth transforms so the same enquiry visibly becomes checked information and then completed outputs. Allow enough time to read each meaningful state.
+
+Buttons:
+160–220ms colour and border transitions, plus a subtle pressed state. Keep labels stable.
+
+Navigation:
+Smooth anchor movement with an appropriate sticky-header offset. Section headings must remain visible after navigation.
+
+Exceptions:
+Stop the motion clearly. Show the reason and next decision. Use wording and iconography as well as colour.
+
+Avoid:
+Scroll hijacking, endless loops, floating particles, decorative AI brains, cursor effects, animated counters without evidence and repeated letter-by-letter headings.
+
+Respect reduced-motion preferences. Provide a readable static sequence or manual steps, plus pause/replay for the animated demonstration.
+
+Keep layout dimensions stable while states change.
+
+9. MAKE THE CONTACT JOURNEY HONEST
+
+The current booking page is an enquiry form rather than an immediate time-slot booking experience.
+
+Use “Request a 15-minute fit call” consistently unless a real scheduler is implemented.
+
+Explain the next step before submission. Match the stated response time to the actual operating commitment and confirmation message.
+
+Final CTA copy:
+
+Heading:
+Which task keeps coming back?
+
+Body:
+Tell us what your team keeps chasing. In a 15-minute call, we’ll identify a candidate workflow, the main constraints and whether Twinthos could be a fit.
+
+Button:
+Request a fit call
+
+Keep the £999 operations audit as a clearly explained secondary route. State its deliverables, payment timing and credit conditions consistently.
+
+10. REVIEW BEFORE RELEASE
+
+Prepare a private preview with revised copy and working motion.
+
+Check:
+• A first-time visitor can explain the service after one screen.
+• The primary CTA is visible at common laptop sizes.
+• Mobile has readable text and no horizontal overflow.
+• The successful workflow and exception both make causal sense.
+• Review, pause, replay and reset controls work.
+• Pending approval never appears as completed work.
+• Pricing and pilot language agree everywhere.
+• Every numerical claim has supporting evidence.
+• Reduced motion and keyboard use work.
+• The form’s wording matches what submission actually does.
+
+Then conduct two reviews:
+
+Red team:
+Identify anything vague, implausible, contradictory or insufficient to justify the fee.
+
+Blue team:
+Resolve those objections through clearer scope, stronger demonstration, genuine evidence and simpler copy.
+
+Deliver the private preview, a concise change log and any remaining commercial decisions. Follow the existing approval process before public deployment.
+
+The intended reaction:
+“I understand the work Twinthos could take on, what I would receive, where I remain in control, and why I would pay for someone to manage it.”

+HERMES: TWINTHOS COMMERCIAL REWRITE AND COMPLETE DESIGN REVISION
+
+Use the following as one coherent implementation brief.
+
+The objective is to make a suitable buyer think:
+
+“That is happening in my business. I understand what Twinthos would take responsibility for. I can see how it works, what my team still does and how we would judge whether it is worth the fee.”
+
+Build a private preview. Preserve the existing requirement for approval before public deployment.
+
+STRATEGIC FOUNDATION
+
+Primary audience:
+Owners and operations leaders in established service businesses with enough recurring work to justify a managed service.
+
+Their immediate problems:
+• Enquiries wait while the team handles other work.
+• Quotes go out without a clear next action.
+• Client work stalls because information is missing.
+• Staff copy updates between systems.
+• Managers keep checking whether the next step happened.
+
+Their deeper concerns:
+• Opportunities may be slipping away.
+• Skilled people are spending time on repeated coordination.
+• The owner remains the fallback for unfinished work.
+• Adding another tool could create another thing to manage.
+• An AI implementation could require more supervision than it removes.
+
+These are positioning hypotheses drawn from the project documents. Do not present them as findings from Twinthos customer interviews.
+
+Desired outcome:
+Work is followed through, the record is current and the right person receives the decisions that need them.
+
+Brand purpose:
+Give people the freedom to build, serve and decide by taking responsibility for recurring operational work.
+
+Brand enemy:
+Operational drag: the repeated chasing, checking and updating that keeps a business dependent on someone’s attention.
+
+Commercial mechanism:
+Understand the process. Define the job. Connect the tools. Test the actions. Manage the work.
+
+Keep this distinction clear:
+An illustrative example explains the service.
+A verified customer result demonstrates performance.
+Neither should be presented as the other.
+
+FIRST: REPAIR THE INCOMPLETE IMPLEMENTATION
+
+The inspected deployment contains new demo HTML with older behaviour.

+• Missing styles for the new demo cards and containers.

+ok now audit twinthos website and make deep changes using the 5.5 model opus - 
+Create a breathtaking 15-second motion design showreel for the Twinthos website. It must work as a persuasive brand advert and demonstrate exceptional motion design craft.
+
+The viewer must immediately understand:
+Twinthos handles routine business work across connected tools, so people can focus on customers, decisions and growth.
+
+Deliver the finished animation, not just a storyboard or a description.
+
+FIRST: UNDERSTAND THE BRAND
+Read all available Twinthos project files and inspect the current website. Base the animation on verified capabilities. Do not invent performance figures, customer results, integrations or product interfaces.
+
+Use a small specialist team:
+? Brand strategist: clarify the customer?s pain and the promise.
+? Creative director: develop one distinctive visual idea.
+? Motion designer: create choreography, transitions and rhythm.
+? Engineer: deliver smooth, responsive website playback.
+Then run a red-team critique and blue-team revision before final delivery.
+
+CREATIVE IDEA: ?THE WORK MOVES. YOU MOVE FORWARD.?
+
+Build the film around a signature motion wheel: an elegant circular sequence of business tasks.
+
+At first, a person must move every task forward individually. Then Twinthos connects the sequence and handles the routine follow-through. The person remains responsible for important decisions.
+
+The wheel must explain the product through movement. It must never resemble a loading spinner, generic dashboard or decorative sci-fi interface.
+
+VISUAL STORY: EXACTLY 15 SECONDS
+
+0?3 SECONDS ? RECOGNITION
+Open in motion immediately.
+
+Large, precise typography:
+?Still chasing the work??
+
+Three clear task fragments interrupt one another:
+?Follow up.?
+?Update records.?
+?Send updates.?
+
+Show the friction through stop-start movement, repeated handoffs and interrupted progress. Keep the composition controlled and readable.
+
+3?6 SECONDS ? THE TRANSFORMATION
+Introduce:
+?Meet Twinthos.?
+
+The scattered task fragments connect into one beautifully engineered motion wheel.
+
+Make this the signature moment: the same elements reorganise through a continuous, surprising transformation. No arbitrary scene cut. The viewer should feel the change from fragmented effort to coordinated execution.
+
+6?11 SECONDS ? SHOW THE WORK GETTING DONE
+The wheel advances through three unmistakable actions:
+
+?Follow-up sent.?
+?Record updated.?
+?Update delivered.?
+
+Each action visibly completes before the next moves forward. Use deliberate camera movement, typography and changes in scale to guide attention.
+
+Make clear that this happens within agreed rules. A distinct decision point pauses for human input:
+?Your approval.?
+
+Keep the explanation simple enough to understand with sound off and on a phone.
+
+11?15 SECONDS ? THE HUMAN PAYOFF
+The completed workflow resolves into a calm, confident brand composition.
+
+Main line:
+?Less chasing. More progress.?
+
+Supporting line:
+?Managed AI. Human oversight.?
+
+TWINTHOS
+?See what we can take off your plate.?
+
+End with a subtle visual connection back to the opening so the sequence loops gracefully. Keep the final message readable for at least two seconds.
+
+ART DIRECTION
+Aim for the restraint, precision and production quality of a world-class technology campaign, with an original Twinthos identity.
+
+? Deep black, warm white and a decisive emerald accent.
+? Large, beautifully kerned typography readable on mobile.
+? Strong composition and purposeful negative space.
+? Exceptional control of timing, acceleration, deceleration and visual hierarchy.
+? Sophisticated transitions built from shared shapes, words and movement.
+? One coherent visual language throughout.
+? Subtle ?Human Architect? signature on the end frame.
+
+Avoid generic AI imagery, robots, glowing brains, neon circuitry, floating glass cards, stock templates, random particles and gratuitous 3D.
+
+ENGAGEMENT AND MOTION
+Create impact through contrast:
+interruption ? coordination
+effort ? completion
+busy movement ? confident stillness
+
+Use anticipation, staggered timing, elegant overshoot and satisfying moments of alignment. Every movement must direct attention, explain a relationship or reinforce the brand.
+
+Do not confuse speed with excitement. Give the viewer time to understand the important moments. No flashing, frantic zooms or unreadable kinetic text.
+
+SOUND
+Create restrained, original sound design that supports each transformation and completion. No cheesy corporate soundtrack.
+
+The website version must autoplay muted and communicate fully without audio. Offer sound only through an explicit user control.
+
+WEBSITE DELIVERY
+Inspect the existing website stack and choose the most reliable implementation for this animation.
+
+? Produce desktop and mobile compositions, not a cropped desktop export.
+? Preserve readable typography and safe margins at every size.
+? Prioritise smooth playback and fast page loading.
+? Provide a strong static poster and reduced-motion alternative.
+? Pause when off-screen.
+? Keep the final website CTA accessible as real HTML.
+? Deliver editable source files and optimised final assets.
+? Integrate into the existing website if project access is available.
+? Preview privately; do not publish publicly without approval.
+
+QUALITY GATE
+Review the actual rendered animation, not just the code or storyboard.
+
+Red team:
+Can a first-time visitor explain what Twinthos does?
+Is the manual-to-managed transformation unmistakable?
+Does anything feel generic, confusing, cheap or unnecessarily busy?
+Can every essential line be read on a phone?
+Are any claims unsupported?
+
+Blue team:
+Resolve each issue, render again and verify the finished result on desktop and mobile.
+
+The ambition: a visitor stops scrolling because of the craft, keeps watching because the transformation is satisfying, and finishes knowing what Twinthos can do for their business.

# 2026-09-30 16:48:36.417835
+Implement a complete visual, copy and motion redesign of twinthos.com using the following brief.
+
+Deliver the updated website, a working hero motion wheel, a complete 45-second brand advert and a 24-second silent hero cut.
+
+Use the existing project. Preserve working routes, forms, approved brand assets and verified commercial terms. Produce a private working preview. Do not publish publicly without approval.
+
+This brief incorporates the live website audit conducted on 30 September 2026. Move directly into implementation, checking current code and operational evidence where needed.
+
+1. THE CENTRAL IDEA
+
+?The work moves. You move on.?
+
+The website must explain:
+
+WHY Twinthos exists.
+WHAT problem the customer recognises.
+HOW Twinthos handles the work.
+WHY operating outside office hours matters.
+WHAT remains under human control.
+WHY the managed service deserves its price.
+WHAT the visitor should do next.
+
+The experience should sell the human outcome first, demonstrate the mechanism second and explain the service details third.
+
+2. BRAND POSITIONING
+
+WHY:
+People should have time for the customers, decisions and lives that need them.
+
+ENEMY:
+Operational drag. The repeated chasing, checking and copying that keeps useful work waiting for somebody?s attention.
+
+HOW:
+Twinthos connects the agreed tools, defines the rules, runs the workflow and manages it over time.
+
+WHAT:
+A managed AI service for recurring business operations.
+
+PROMISE:
+?Work handled. Time returned.?
+
+CREATIVE EXPRESSION:
+?The work moves. You move on.?
+
+24/7 operation is an important operating advantage. Its human value is that people can step away while agreed routine work continues.
+
+Combine this advantage with managed delivery, connected tools, clear responsibilities and evidence. Do not present 24/7 alone as a unique invention.
+
+3. LIVE WEBSITE PROBLEMS TO CORRECT
+
+The current hero film sits above the main headline and explanation.
+
+At the audited desktop viewport of 1363 × 936:
+The headline started approximately 723px down.
+The main hero CTA started approximately 1,442px down.
+The header CTA remained visible.
+
+Rebuild the composition so the main value proposition and action are visible immediately.
+
+The embedded film remained on its poster in the audit browser after reloading, although the actual video file played when opened directly. Diagnose the embedded playback using real loading and playback events. Do not assume the file is missing or invent a technical cause.
+
+Scrolling produced temporarily empty or blurred sections before text became readable. Remove these reveal states from essential content.
+
+The current copy repeats the authority line, human decisions and follow-through across too many sections. Consolidate these ideas.
+
+Replace ?Everything else, we carry.? It overstates a service sold within an agreed scope.
+
+Replace the pricing headline ?One service. One price. One way in.? The site offers both a managed service and an optional operations audit.
+
+The site promises a ?named operator? without identifying that person in the inspected copy. Provide the approved public operator identity and genuine experience.
+
+The demo?s approval state contains both ?Play again? and ?Replay.? Simplify its controls while preserving the working demonstration.
+
+The footer phrase ?Agentic platforms, on-call, end-to-end? is vague and may imply support availability that the service does not offer.
+
+4. CLAIMS AND EVIDENCE
+
+24/7:
+
+Use ?Routine work, running 24/7? only where the actual deployment, scheduling, permissions and service scope support it.
+
+Verify this against the running implementation before publication.
+
+Infrastructure capability alone does not establish Twinthos uptime.
+
+If verified, explain:
+?Agreed workflows can run day and night. Human support is available during the agreed support hours.?
+
+If unverified, use:
+?Routine work, handled on schedule.?
+
+Remove unsupported overnight completion claims until they are established.
+
+Do not equate 24/7 operation with:
+Guaranteed uninterrupted availability.
+Instant execution.
+24-hour human support.
+Unlimited throughput.
+Permission to contact customers at inappropriate hours.
+
+Respect approved sending windows and approval rules.
+
+Integrations:
+
+Composio publishes a catalogue of 1,000+ toolkits. A toolkit is not the same as one individual action, and catalogue availability does not prove that Twinthos has deployed and tested every connection.
+
+If Composio is confirmed in the implementation, use:
+?Built with access to Composio?s catalogue of 1,000+ toolkits. Connections and supported actions are confirmed for your workflow.?
+
+Otherwise use:
+?Connect the tools your work already moves through.?
+
+Do not inflate ?1,000+? into an unsupported ?thousands of integrations.?
+
+Results:
+
+Separate:
+Verified service terms.
+External product capabilities.
+Illustrative workflows.
+Measured Twinthos results.
+
+Do not invent testimonials, customers, certifications, saved hours, revenue increases, response times, staff replacement ratios or performance guarantees.
+
+Third-party research documents and Dewey examples are background material, not proof of Twinthos results.
+
+5. HERO COMPOSITION AND EXACT COPY
+
+Desktop:
+Create one integrated composition.
+Text and CTA on the left.
+A large, readable motion wheel on the right.
+
+The wheel must explain the service without pushing the headline and primary action below the opening viewport.
+
+Mobile:
+Headline.
+Short explanation.
+Primary action.
+Motion wheel.
+
+Recompose for mobile. Do not shrink a desktop dashboard into an unreadable thumbnail.
+
+Eyebrow:
+?MANAGED AI FOR BUSINESS OPERATIONS?
+
+Headline:
+?Your people have better work to do.?
+
+Supporting line:
+?Routine work, running 24/7.?
+
+Apply the verification rule above.
+
+Body:
+?Twinthos builds and manages AI workflows for enquiries, follow-ups, document requests and record updates. The work moves through your existing tools. Your people get more room for customers, delivery and decisions.?
+
+Primary CTA:
+?Find my first workflow?
+
+Destination:
+The existing call-request page.
+
+Microcopy:
+?A 15-minute call to identify the work worth handing over.?
+
+Secondary CTA:
+?Watch the 45-second story?
+
+Open the full advert on request.
+
+Price qualifier:
+?£5,000/month · Agreed scope · Three-month minimum · Excluding VAT where applicable?
+
+Keep the main CTA independent of video playback.
+
+Replace the logo-only poster with an informative frame showing:
+Twinthos.
+A recognisable task.
+The wheel.
+A clear result.
+
+6. HOMEPAGE SECTION: THE PROBLEM
+
+Headline:
+?The work is small. The interruption isn?t.?
+
+Body:
+?An unanswered enquiry. A missing document. A record waiting to be updated. Each creates another check, another message and another interruption.?
+
+Show three large editorial task statements:
+?Check the inbox.?
+?Chase the missing detail.?
+?Update the record.?
+
+Resolve to:
+?That is operational drag.?
+
+Supporting copy:
+?Twinthos takes on agreed recurring steps so your team can stay with the work that needs them.?
+
+Visual treatment:
+Fragmented requests become an organised sequence.
+
+Make the before and after visibly different through composition, state and movement.
+
+Avoid a long list of dimmed, struck-through questions.
+
+Do not portray employees as lazy or incompetent. Show the burden of fragmented work.
+
+7. HOMEPAGE SECTION: AFTER-HOURS VALUE
+
+Headline:
+?Close the laptop. Keep the work moving.?
+
+Body:
+?Agreed workflows can receive enquiries, request missing information and update connected records outside office hours. Your team returns to a clearer next step.?
+
+Publish this copy only for supported actions and operating hours.
+
+Visual:
+An evening enquiry progresses through the wheel.
+A morning summary separates completed work from decisions awaiting review.
+
+Readable operating note:
+?Automated operating hours and human support hours are agreed separately.?
+
+Do not hide this distinction in tiny text.
+
+8. HOMEPAGE SECTION: ONE COMPLETE JOB
+
+Headline:
+?One enquiry. A clear next step.?
+
+Body:
+?See an enquiry arrive, the missing details requested and the customer record updated. When a decision falls outside the agreed rules, your team takes over.?
+
+Use the existing enquiry demo and approval behaviour as the functional foundation.
+
+Preserve:
+The sample-data disclosure.
+The human approval example.
+The ability to inspect individual steps.
+
+Improve:
+Larger text.
+A visible step count.
+Clear completion states.
+One Play/Pause control.
+One Replay control.
+Keyboard operation.
+An inspectable approval state.
+
+Caption:
+?Illustrative workflow. Sample data. Timing compressed.?
+
+Show a customer reply as a separate incoming event. Do not imply Twinthos invents missing customer information.
+
+Show the result of an action before marking it complete. A tool request being sent does not establish that the business outcome succeeded.
+
+9. HOMEPAGE SECTION: CONNECTIONS
+
+Headline:
+?The tools stay. The handoffs change.?
+
+Body:
+?Email, calendars, customer records and documents already hold the information. Twinthos connects the agreed steps between them.?
+
+Show information moving between three or four relevant systems.
+
+Use verified app connections and clear category labels.
+
+An integration count should support the explanation rather than replace it.
+
+Do not use a giant logo cloud or imply partnerships, customer relationships or universal compatibility through logos.
+
+If verified, include the qualified Composio catalogue statement from the claims section.
+
+10. HOMEPAGE SECTION: MANAGED DELIVERY
+
+Headline:
+?A defined job. Someone responsible for keeping it working.?
+
+Three editorial rows:
+
+?Define the work.?
+?We map the process, agree the outcome and set the decisions that stay with your team.?
+
+?Build and test.?
+?We connect the agreed systems and test routine cases and exceptions.?
+
+?Run and maintain.?
+?We monitor the workflow, maintain its connections and review what it handles.?
+
+Control statement:
+?Your rules. Your decisions.?
+
+Supporting copy:
+?The workflow acts within agreed permissions. Requests outside those rules come to the right person with the relevant context.?
+
+Keep the authority-line concept as one supporting explanation. Reduce repetitions elsewhere.
+
+Identify the operator using an approved public name, genuine biography and real portrait if available.
+
+Do not invent credentials or a larger team.
+
+11. HOMEPAGE SECTION: BUSINESS CASE
+
+Headline:
+?Start with a workflow worth £5,000 a month.?
+
+Body:
+?We look at how often the work happens, how much attention it takes and what improves when it is handled. Your proposal sets out the scope, measures and assumptions.?
+
+Three measurement labels:
+?Human handling time?
+?Completed work and exceptions?
+?Cost and business value?
+
+Where available, show:
+A genuine redacted output.
+An activity record.
+A measured pilot.
+A real before-and-after workflow.
+
+Include the task, measurement period, sample, remaining human review and limitations.
+
+If evidence is unavailable, show an explicitly illustrative example and explain what will be measured.
+
+Keep recovered time, actual cash savings and additional profit separate.
+
+Do not double-count the same benefit.
+
+No calculator.
+No invented salary comparison.
+No animated savings counter.
+No unsupported employee-replacement claim.
+
+12. HOMEPAGE SECTION: PRICE AND NEXT STEP
+
+Headline:
+?One managed workflow. A clear scope.?
+
+Offer:
+?Managed service?
+?£5,000/month?

+
+Included:
+Design and implementation.
+Agreed system connections.
+Testing and exception handling.
+Monitoring and maintenance.
+Agreed changes.
+Operational reporting and support.
+
+Terms:
+?Three-month minimum. £15,000 in service fees over the minimum term, excluding VAT where applicable and agreed extras. Billing starts when the workflow is live. Thirty days? notice after the minimum term.?
+
+Confirm the wording against the current service terms before publication.
+
+Secondary offer:
+?Want the plan first??

+?A written assessment of the work, the opportunity and a proposed starting scope. You keep the plan.?
+
+Credit statement:

+
+Closing headline:
+?What should stop landing back on your desk??

+Body:

+CTA:

+Subtle, readable signature:

+Present the managed service and optional audit as distinct routes.

+Booking:

+Clarify whether the two-working-day response commitment concerns the submitted request or the post-call follow-up.
+Preserve the actual commitment unless the operator changes it.
+Do not submit a live enquiry during testing.

+14. VISUAL DESIGN SYSTEM
+

+Body approximately 18?20px desktop and at least 16px mobile.

+15. MOTION WHEEL DESIGN
+
+The wheel must explain a managed operating cycle.
+
+Centre:
+Twinthos wordmark.
+?Managed AI?
+
+Six stationary stages:
+Receive.
+Check.
+Act.
+Verify.
+Record.
+Report.
+
+Stage meanings:
+
+Receive:
+An enquiry or scheduled task arrives.
+
+Check:
+Required information and agreed rules are checked.
+
+Act:
+An approved business action is performed.
+
+Verify:
+The response or result is checked.
+
+Record:
+The connected record is updated.
+
+Report:
+Completed work and exceptions become visible to the team.
+
+Keep labels upright and stationary.
+
+Move one task marker clockwise.
+Illuminate the active stage.
+Display one large evidence card beside the wheel.
+Completed stages gain a tick and a readable state.
+
+Outer ring:
+A restrained 24-hour dial.
+Only four labels: 00, 06, 12, 18.
+A time indicator communicates evening, night and morning.
+
+Human decision branch:
+?Needs your decision.?
+
+Place it outside the automatic path.
+
+An exception pauses the affected action.
+Do not show approval happening automatically.
+
+Use emerald, a tick and ?Completed? for success.
+
+Use amber, a pause symbol and ?Awaiting approval? for human review.
+
+Do not rely on colour alone.
+
+Do not label illustrative activity ?Live.?
+
+16. COMPLETE 45-SECOND BRAND ADVERT
+
+Creative idea:
+?The work moves. You move on.?
+
+00?05 SECONDS: WHY
+
+Warm-paper background.
+
+Large headline:
+?Your people have better work to do.?
+
+Three words arrive with deliberate spacing:
+?Customers.?
+?Decisions.?
+?Life.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?Your people?s time belongs with customers, decisions, and life beyond the business.?
+
+The first frame must communicate a human benefit. Avoid a long logo introduction.
+
+05?10 SECONDS: THE PROBLEM
+
+Shift to ink-dark.
+
+Three large task cards interrupt the composition:
+?Reply to the enquiry.?
+?Chase the document.?
+?Update the record.?
+
+An office-hours marker reaches 17:30.
+One unresolved card remains.
+
+Headline:
+?Routine work keeps asking for attention.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?But routine work keeps asking for it. Another check. Another chase. Another update.?
+
+10?14 SECONDS: INTRODUCE TWINTHOS
+
+The same task cards align into the wheel.
+The T mark resolves at its centre.
+
+Headline:
+?Give the routine a system.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?Twinthos builds and manages the workflows that take those recurring steps forward.?
+
+The transformation should feel precise.
+Avoid explosions, particles and camera fly-throughs.
+
+14?25 SECONDS: DEMONSTRATE THE WORK
+
+One illustrative enquiry moves through the cycle.
+
+Receive:
+?New enquiry received.?
+
+Check:
+?Site details missing.?
+
+Act:
+?Approved information request sent.?
+
+Show a distinct incoming event:
+?Customer supplies the details.?
+
+Verify:
+?Required details present.?
+
+Record:
+?Customer record updated.?
+
+Report:
+?Ready for your team.?
+
+Show one evidence card at a time.
+
+Keep the enquiry?s identity consistent so viewers understand that each action belongs to the same job.

+Voiceover:

+25?31 SECONDS: THE 24/7 ADVANTAGE
+
+Reveal the outer clock.
+
+Move through evening, night and morning.
+
+The workflow continues while the surrounding composition changes with the time of day.

+Headline:
+?Routine work, running 24/7.?
+
+Secondary:
+?Within your agreed rules.?

+?Agreed work can keep moving after hours, ready for the people who take it further.?

+?Routine work, handled on schedule.?
+
+Fallback voiceover:
+?Agreed work moves on schedule, ready for the people who take it further.?
+

+The clock is an explanatory illustration, not an uptime measurement.

+31?36 SECONDS: HUMAN CONTROL

+?Record current.?
+?Decision ready.?

+18. MOTION CRAFT
+
+Use two-dimensional editorial motion.
+Precise line work.
+Real typography.
+Warm white, ink, emerald and amber.
+
+Every movement should explain:
+A task arriving.
+A rule being checked.
+An action happening.
+A result being confirmed.
+A record changing.
+A decision reaching the right person.
+
+Accelerate between stages, then hold the result still.
+
+Transitions generally 350?650ms.
+
+Give main messages approximately 2.5?4 seconds wherever the edit permits.
+
+Check reading time at normal playback speed.
+Simplify text instead of accelerating unreadable copy.
+
+Show no more than one dominant headline and one active evidence card at a time.
+
+The wheel must not resemble a loading spinner.
+
+19. MOBILE MOTION
+
+Create a dedicated portrait composition.
+
+Keep the wheel large.
+Keep stage labels short and upright.
+Place the active evidence card beneath it.
+Show only four clock labels.
+
+Do not squeeze six long descriptions around a small circle.
+
+Do not crop the desktop film and describe that as a mobile version.
+
+20. INTEGRATION SCALE IN THE STORY
+
+Keep the qualified 1,000+ toolkit message in the adjacent website section or an expanded film version.
+
+Use a dated primary source and distinguish catalogue availability from deployed Twinthos support.
+
+Do not overload the 45-second advert with a second wheel of logos.
+
+The viewer should understand the work before being shown the breadth of the ecosystem.

+21. AUDIO
+
+Hero:
+Silent.
+
+Full advert:
+User-initiated sound.
+Calm human voiceover.
+Restrained movement cues.

+22. ACCESSIBILITY AND PERFORMANCE

+Provide:
+Pause.
+Replay.
+Keyboard controls.
+Visible focus.
+Reduced-motion presentation.
+Static explanation.
+
+Pause off-screen animation.
+
+No autoplay audio.
+No scroll hijacking.
+No essential content dependent on video loading or scroll-animation success.

+1. THE CENTRAL IDEA
+
+?The work moves. You move on.?
+
+The website must explain:
+
+WHY Twinthos exists.
+WHAT problem the customer recognises.
+HOW Twinthos handles the work.
+WHY operating outside office hours matters.
+WHAT remains under human control.
+WHY the managed service deserves its price.
+WHAT the visitor should do next.
+
+The experience should sell the human outcome first, demonstrate the mechanism second and explain the service details third.
+
+2. BRAND POSITIONING
+
+WHY:
+People should have time for the customers, decisions and lives that need them.
+
+ENEMY:
+Operational drag. The repeated chasing, checking and copying that keeps useful work waiting for somebody?s attention.
+
+HOW:
+Twinthos connects the agreed tools, defines the rules, runs the workflow and manages it over time.
+
+WHAT:
+A managed AI service for recurring business operations.
+
+PROMISE:
+?Work handled. Time returned.?
+
+CREATIVE EXPRESSION:
+?The work moves. You move on.?
+
+24/7 operation is an important operating advantage. Its human value is that people can step away while agreed routine work continues.
+
+Combine this advantage with managed delivery, connected tools, clear responsibilities and evidence. Do not present 24/7 alone as a unique invention.
+
+3. LIVE WEBSITE PROBLEMS TO CORRECT
+
+The current hero film sits above the main headline and explanation.
+
+At the audited desktop viewport of 1363 × 936:
+The headline started approximately 723px down.
+The main hero CTA started approximately 1,442px down.
+The header CTA remained visible.
+
+Rebuild the composition so the main value proposition and action are visible immediately.
+
+The embedded film remained on its poster in the audit browser after reloading, although the actual video file played when opened directly. Diagnose the embedded playback using real loading and playback events. Do not assume the file is missing or invent a technical cause.
+
+Scrolling produced temporarily empty or blurred sections before text became readable. Remove these reveal states from essential content.
+
+The current copy repeats the authority line, human decisions and follow-through across too many sections. Consolidate these ideas.
+
+Replace ?Everything else, we carry.? It overstates a service sold within an agreed scope.
+
+Replace the pricing headline ?One service. One price. One way in.? The site offers both a managed service and an optional operations audit.
+
+The site promises a ?named operator? without identifying that person in the inspected copy. Provide the approved public operator identity and genuine experience.
+
+The demo?s approval state contains both ?Play again? and ?Replay.? Simplify its controls while preserving the working demonstration.
+
+The footer phrase ?Agentic platforms, on-call, end-to-end? is vague and may imply support availability that the service does not offer.
+
+4. CLAIMS AND EVIDENCE
+
+24/7:
+
+Use ?Routine work, running 24/7? only where the actual deployment, scheduling, permissions and service scope support it.
+
+Verify this against the running implementation before publication.
+
+Infrastructure capability alone does not establish Twinthos uptime.
+
+If verified, explain:
+?Agreed workflows can run day and night. Human support is available during the agreed support hours.?
+
+If unverified, use:
+?Routine work, handled on schedule.?
+
+Remove unsupported overnight completion claims until they are established.
+
+Do not equate 24/7 operation with:
+Guaranteed uninterrupted availability.
+Instant execution.
+24-hour human support.
+Unlimited throughput.
+Permission to contact customers at inappropriate hours.
+
+Respect approved sending windows and approval rules.
+
+Integrations:
+
+Composio publishes a catalogue of 1,000+ toolkits. A toolkit is not the same as one individual action, and catalogue availability does not prove that Twinthos has deployed and tested every connection.
+
+If Composio is confirmed in the implementation, use:
+?Built with access to Composio?s catalogue of 1,000+ toolkits. Connections and supported actions are confirmed for your workflow.?
+
+Otherwise use:
+?Connect the tools your work already moves through.?
+
+Do not inflate ?1,000+? into an unsupported ?thousands of integrations.?
+
+Results:
+
+Separate:
+Verified service terms.
+External product capabilities.
+Illustrative workflows.
+Measured Twinthos results.
+
+Do not invent testimonials, customers, certifications, saved hours, revenue increases, response times, staff replacement ratios or performance guarantees.
+
+Third-party research documents and Dewey examples are background material, not proof of Twinthos results.

+15. MOTION WHEEL DESIGN
+
+The wheel must explain a managed operating cycle.
+
+Centre:
+Twinthos wordmark.
+?Managed AI?
+
+Six stationary stages:
+Receive.
+Check.
+Act.
+Verify.
+Record.
+Report.
+
+Stage meanings:
+
+Receive:
+An enquiry or scheduled task arrives.
+
+Check:
+Required information and agreed rules are checked.
+
+Act:
+An approved business action is performed.
+
+Verify:
+The response or result is checked.
+
+Record:
+The connected record is updated.
+
+Report:
+Completed work and exceptions become visible to the team.
+
+Keep labels upright and stationary.
+
+Move one task marker clockwise.
+Illuminate the active stage.
+Display one large evidence card beside the wheel.
+Completed stages gain a tick and a readable state.
+
+Outer ring:
+A restrained 24-hour dial.
+Only four labels: 00, 06, 12, 18.
+A time indicator communicates evening, night and morning.
+
+Human decision branch:
+?Needs your decision.?
+
+Place it outside the automatic path.
+
+An exception pauses the affected action.
+Do not show approval happening automatically.
+
+Use emerald, a tick and ?Completed? for success.
+
+Use amber, a pause symbol and ?Awaiting approval? for human review.
+
+Do not rely on colour alone.
+
+Do not label illustrative activity ?Live.?

+Move through evening, night and morning.
+
+The workflow continues while the surrounding composition changes with the time of day.
+
+Headline:
+?Routine work, running 24/7.?
+
+Secondary:
+?Within your agreed rules.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?Agreed work can keep moving after hours, ready for the people who take it further.?
+
+This scene requires verified Twinthos operating scope.
+
+If unverified, replace the headline with:
+?Routine work, handled on schedule.?
+
+Fallback voiceover:
+?Agreed work moves on schedule, ready for the people who take it further.?
+
+Show record processing overnight and outbound communication within approved sending windows.
+
+The clock is an explanatory illustration, not an uptime measurement.
+
+31?36 SECONDS: HUMAN CONTROL
+
+Introduce a separate exception:
+?15% discount requested.?
+
+The card branches out to:
+?Needs your decision.?
+
+The affected task pauses.
+No price changes.
+No offer is sent.
+
+Headline:
+?You keep the decisions.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?When a request needs your judgement, it waits for your decision.?
+
+36?40 SECONDS: THE HUMAN OUTCOME
+
+Resolve into a calm morning summary:
+?Details collected.?
+?Record current.?
+?Decision ready.?
+
+The fragmented task stack from the opening has become one clear view.
+
+Headline:
+?Work handled. Time returned.?
+
+Voiceover:
+?Less chasing. More room for the work that needs you.?
+
+Optional licensed real footage may show someone focused on a customer. The graphic story must remain understandable without it.
+
+40?45 SECONDS: CLOSE
+
+Warm-paper end frame.
+
+Twinthos wordmark.
+
+Headline:
+?What should stop landing back on your desk??
+
+Action:
+?Find my first workflow?
+
+Supporting line:
+?A 15-minute conversation.?
+
+URL:
+?twinthos.com?
+
+Voiceover:
+?Twinthos. Work handled. Time returned. Find your first workflow.?
+
+Hold the end card for five seconds.
+
+On the website, place a real clickable CTA outside the exported film.
+
+17. 24-SECOND SILENT HERO CUT
+
+Use the same visual assets.
+
+00?03:
+?Your people have better work to do.?
+Show one unresolved task.
+
+03?06:
+The task aligns into the Twinthos wheel.
+?Give the routine a system.?
+
+06?14:
+Show three readable result states:
+?Details requested.?
+?Reply recorded.?
+?Next step ready.?
+
+Acknowledge the incoming customer reply before showing it recorded.
+
+14?18:
+Reveal the clock.
+?Routine work, running 24/7.?
+
+Use the verified fallback when necessary.
+
+18?21:
+An exception branches out.
+?You keep the decisions.?
+
+21?24:
+?Work handled. Time returned.?
+
+Return to the opening through a restrained dissolve.
+
+Do not reverse processing or visually undo completed work.
+
+Keep Pause/Play available outside the film.
+
+The static poster must communicate the offer without playback.
+
+18. MOTION CRAFT
+
+Use two-dimensional editorial motion.
+Precise line work.
+Real typography.
+Warm white, ink, emerald and amber.
+
+Every movement should explain:
+A task arriving.
+A rule being checked.
+An action happening.
+A result being confirmed.
+A record changing.
+A decision reaching the right person.
+
+Accelerate between stages, then hold the result still.
+
+Transitions generally 350?650ms.
+
+Give main messages approximately 2.5?4 seconds wherever the edit permits.
+
+Check reading time at normal playback speed.
+Simplify text instead of accelerating unreadable copy.
+
+Show no more than one dominant headline and one active evidence card at a time.
+
+The wheel must not resemble a loading spinner.
+
+19. MOBILE MOTION
+
+Create a dedicated portrait composition.
+
+Keep the wheel large.
+Keep stage labels short and upright.
+Place the active evidence card beneath it.
+Show only four clock labels.
+
+Do not squeeze six long descriptions around a small circle.
+
+Do not crop the desktop film and describe that as a mobile version.
+
+20. INTEGRATION SCALE IN THE STORY
+
+Keep the qualified 1,000+ toolkit message in the adjacent website section or an expanded film version.
+
+Use a dated primary source and distinguish catalogue availability from deployed Twinthos support.
+
+Do not overload the 45-second advert with a second wheel of logos.
+
+The viewer should understand the work before being shown the breadth of the ecosystem.
+
+21. AUDIO
+
+Hero:
+Silent.
+
+Full advert:
+User-initiated sound.
+Calm human voiceover.
+Restrained movement cues.
+One subtle confirmation sound on completion.
+
+Avoid notification barrages, alarms and hype-trailer music.
+
+The film must communicate fully without audio.
+
+Provide captions and a transcript.
+
+22. ACCESSIBILITY AND PERFORMANCE
+
+Keep website copy and controls as accessible HTML.
+
+Build the wheel with crisp SVG/HTML animation where appropriate, or use a properly encoded film with a semantic text equivalent.
+
+Handle blocked autoplay with an obvious Play button and informative poster.
+
+Provide:
+Pause.
+Replay.
+Keyboard controls.
+Visible focus.
+Reduced-motion presentation.
+Static explanation.
+
+Pause off-screen animation.
+
+No autoplay audio.
+No scroll hijacking.
+No essential content dependent on video loading or scroll-animation success.
+
+Use meaningful loading and failure states.
+
+23. EXPORTS AND DELIVERABLES
+
+Deliver:
+
+Updated private website preview.
+
+Working hero motion wheel.
+
+45-second brand advert.
+
+24-second silent hero cut.
+
+1920×1080 landscape master.
+
+Separately recomposed 1080×1440 portrait version.
+
+H.264 MP4 for broad compatibility.
+
+Optional WebM alternative.
+
+Web-optimised exports with fast-start metadata.
+
+Poster images for each layout.
+
+Editable vector and text source.
+
+Timing specification.
+
+Caption file and transcript.
+
+Matching-width before-and-after website screenshots.
+
+A concise list of material changes and unresolved claim evidence.
+
+24. FINAL VALIDATION
+
+Inspect actual rendered pages at:
+390px.
+768px.
+1366px.
+1920px.
+
+Also check:
+Zoom.
+Reduced motion.
+Keyboard operation.
+Text contrast.
+Clipped headlines.
+Horizontal overflow.
+CTA destinations.
+Form errors.
+Media loading.
+Autoplay fallback.
+Pause and replay.
+Static poster clarity.
+
+Do not submit a real enquiry while testing.
+
+Report actual performance measurements. Do not invent scores.
+
+Review all pages for consistent service terms, operating hours and support hours.
+
+Watch the film at its actual website display size, with sound off.
+
+A first-time viewer should be able to explain:
+Why Twinthos exists.
+What problem it handles.
+What work moves between tools.
+What continues outside office hours.
+Where a person decides.
